===PAGE 142=== (layout, 25 lines)
   1|        Below is the FY 2023 Capital Plan being recommended for approval at the Annual Town Meeting:
   2| ARTICLE 7.   To see if the Town will vote to approve a reorganization of the Public Works Department in
   3| accordance with a reorganization plan entitled “Administrative Organization Plan,” dated April 5, 2022, and
   4| accompanying message of the Town Manager, both on file at the office of the Town Clerk and Select Board
   5| and posted on the town website, under the authority provided for in Article 5, Section 1(b) of the Town
   6| Charter; or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Town Manager)
   7| FINANCE  COMMITTEE  and  SELECT  BOARD  recommend  Approval.                                   VOTED  (Yes-136,  No-28)  to
   8| approve the Administrative Organization Plan as presented by the Town Manager and as provided for
   9| in Article 5, Section 1 (b) of the Town Charter:
  10| It has been a mutually agreed upon goal of the Town Manager and Select Board since November 2020 to look at
  11| the feasibility of creating an independent facilities department and improve the long term maintenance and
  12| repair  plan  for  town facilities.  Currently  the  Town Manager  delegates  the  responsibility  of  town facilities,
  13| excluding School and Conservation properties, to the DPW Director. This Administrative Reorganization Plan
  14| proposes to create a new Facilities Director position that will be responsible for all town facilities and grounds
  15| maintenance.
  16| The Town Manager, per the Town Charter Section 4               -4 on the Town Manager’s “Powers and Duties” Paragraph
  17| (i), is “to have full jurisdiction over the rental and use of all town facilities and property except property under
  18| the  control  of  the  school  committee  and  the  conservation  commission;  and  shall  be  responsible  for  the
  19| maintenance and repair of all town buildings and facilities placed under his control by this charter, by-law, vote
  20| of the town or otherwise”.       This has been delegated to the DPW Director per the Town Charter Section 4-3
  21| “Delegation of Power”       that states:  “The town manager may authorize any subordinate officer or employee to
  22| exercise any power or perform any function or duty which is assigned to the office of town manager; provided,
  23| however,  that all acts performed under such delegation shall at all times be deemed to be the acts of the Town
  24| Manager.”
  25|                                                                  142

===PAGE 143=== (layout, 44 lines)
   1| Currently the DPW Department encompasses Highway, Sewer, Cemetery, Parks, Facilities and oversight of the
   2| Trash & Recycling Program. After reviewing the organizational structure of the Department of Public Works
   3| and  the  limited  staff  within  this  department  with  a  wide  range  and  breadth  of  responsibilities                  ,  it  is  my
   4| recommendation to create a new Facilities and Grounds Department headed by this new position.
   5| Charter Process
   6| The Administrative Plan process is outlined in the Town Charter, Article 5, as follows:
   7| 1.  The Select Board shall hold one or more public hearings on the proposal giving notice by publication in a
   8|     local newspaper, which notice shall describe the scope of the proposal and the time and place at which the
   9|     hearing will be held, not less than 7 nor more than 14 days following said publication;
  10| 2.  An organization or reorganization plan shall become effective at the expiration of 60 days following the date
  11|     the proposal is submitted to the town meeting unless the town meeting shall, by a majority vote, within such
  12|     period, vote to disapprove the plan. The town meeting may vote only to approve or to disapprove the                          plan and
  13|     may not vote to amend or to alter it.
  14| 3.  The Town Manager, through the              administrative plan, and subject only to express prohibitions in a general
  15|     law or this charter, may reorganize, consolidate or abolish any town agency, in whole or in part; establish
  16|     such new town agencies as are deemed necessary and to the same extent as provided in section 5                          -1(a) for by-
  17|     laws; and for such purpose may transfer the duties and powers and, so far as is consistent with the use for
  18|     which the funds were voted by the town, transfer the appropriation of one town agency to another; provided,
  19|     however, that no function assigned by this charter to a particular town agency may be discontinued or
  20|     assigned to any other town agency unless specifically authorized by this charter.
  21| 4.  Every reorganization plan submitted by the town manager pursuant to this section shall contain a proposed
  22|     by-law which contains, in detail, such amendments, revisions, repeals or otherwise of existing ordinances as
  23|     may be necessary to accomplish the desired reorganization.
  24| 5.  Such reorganization plan and proposed ordinance shall be accompanied by a message from town manager
  25|     which explains the benefits expected to ensue.
  26| Adherence to Charter Process
  27| 1.  A notice was published in the April 1, 2022 Lunenburg Ledger that a public hearing will be held at the April
  28|     12, 2022 Select Board meeting to review the Administrative Organizational Plan presented by the Town
  29|     Manager, in accordance with Section 5-1 (b) of the Town Charter, regarding the impending creation of the
  30|     position of Facilities Director.
  31| 2.  Relative to #3 above, there are no express prohibitions in the town bylaws or in the Town Charter that would
  32|     not allow for the transfer of facilities and grounds duties from the DPW Director to a newly created Facilities
  33|     Director position. The Charter does not assign the duties of facilities and grounds, exclusive of school and
  34|     conservation, to another agency in town, nor are there any bylaws that relate to the oversight of facilities and
  35|     grounds. The reorganization being sought is defined under Section 4-2, Powers and Duties of the Town
  36|     Manager and is permissible under Section 4-3, Delegation of Authority, as outlined earlier.
  37| 3.  The Town Manager, through the administrative plan, and subject only to express prohibitions in a general
  38|     law or this charter, may reorganize, consolidate or abolish any town agency, in whole or in part; establish
  39|     such new town agencies as are deemed necessary and to the same extent as provided in section 5-1(a) for by-
  40|     laws; and for such purpose may transfer the duties and powers and, so far as is consistent with the use for
  41|     which the funds were voted by the town, transfer the appropriation of one town agency to another; provided,
  42|     however, that no function assigned by this charter to a particular town agency may be discontinued or
  43|     assigned to any other town agency unless specifically authorized by this charter.
  44|                                                                    143

===PAGE 144=== (layout, 50 lines)
   1| Message on the Benefits of Creating Director of Facilities and Grounds Position
   2| The  purpose of this reorganization plan is to create a Director of Facilities and Grounds position that would
   3| oversee, supervise, and facilitate the comprehensive management of all town facilities and grounds. The reason
   4| this is being recommended to town meeting, is that it will improve the long and short term management of our
   5| facilities and grounds with an individual whose background and skills relate to property management, capital
   6| planning, building construction, and grounds maintenance. This position will help the town strategize, prioritize
   7| and coordinate maintenance, repairs, safety, and efficiencies of municipal properties, as well as play a key role in
   8| future use and reuse of properties.
   9| The key benefits of creating a Director of Facilities and Grounds Position:
  10| 1. Hiring a professional level position with a background in facilities management. Facilities management
  11| experience is not typical for Department of Public Works Directors.  An individual with this skill set will enhance
  12| and improve the town’s current and future facilities management plan.
  13| 2. A management level position whose sole focus is long term and short term planning and maintenance of town
  14| facilities and grounds. Since the DPW Director is responsible for multiple different divisions, town facilities
  15| receive only a portion of that position’s focus.
  16| 3. More preventative maintenance and less reactionary maintenance of our facilities and grounds.
  17| 4. Collaborate with the School’s counterpart position on the overall maintenance plan for facilities and grounds
  18| and work together with the Town Manager and Finance Director on capital planning and financing alternatives.
  19| 5.  Directed effort towards the health and safety of all staff and users of town facilities.
  20| 6.  6. Create and maintain a property condition assessment of all town facilities and grounds utilizing Facilities
  21|     Management software.
  22| This position will be appointed by the Town Manager, in accordance with Sections 4-2 (d) of the Town Charter,
  23| with ratification of the appointment to be made by the Select Board.
  24| The Director of Facilities and Grounds will have direct supervision for the following existing positions: Facilities
  25| Superintendent, Facilities Custodian, and Recreation Director. Those positions that are currently in the Laborers
  26| International Union of North America, Public Employees, Local 39 will continue to assist with snow and ice
  27| removal operations, mostly related to town facilities and grounds.
  28| Essential functions of this position:
  29| 1.  Responsible for the planning, organizing, directing and control of all aspects of Town facilities and grounds.
  30|     This will include facility planning, renovation, construction, energy conservation measures and efficiencies,
  31|     preventative and regular maintenance, and repair of all town-owned buildings and grounds
  32| 2.  Responsible for  a  comprehensive  approach to  building maintenance and  repair  coordination,  oversight,
  33|     inspection, oversight of maintenance logs, resolving any warranty issues, establishing facility management
  34|     schedules, and building security.
  35| 3.  Formulates  procedures  related  to  Facilities  and  Grounds  operations,  develops  and  oversees  employee
  36|     schedules, and reviews and advises on daily maintenance issues.
  37| 4.  Ensures all facilities staff attend routine training development for essential functions and the proper handling
  38|     and application of all department equipment and tools; ensures staff obtain required licenses and safety
  39|     trainings.
  40| 5.  Focuses on the optimal facility use by staff, residents, and those who seek to conduct business with or in the
  41|     town to ensure an effective user-experience.
  42| 6.  Works with relevant town departments and elected Park Commission to advise and recommend long term
  43|     plan for all town facilities and grounds. Updates the ten-year capital plan for all facilities and grounds
  44|     projects that meet the Town’s definition of a capital project and are not funded in the operating budget.
  45|     Presents and answers all related questions for said plan and updates the Town Manager on the status of
  46|     approved capital projects as they progress.
  47| 7.  Collaborates with the School Facilities Director to ensure a town-wide approach to facilities management and
  48|     sustainability for all properties. Work with the Town Manager and Finance Director to examine how large
  49|     building projects fit into the town’s overall debt schedule.
  50|                                                                     144

===PAGE 145=== (layout, 49 lines)
   1| 8. Acts as contract administrator for any town building or grounds project including the town facilities cleaning
   2| contract and landscaping contracts.
   3| 9. Serves as the representative for any building or grounds project that requires permitting approval at a board/
   4| committee/commission meeting (i.e. APDC, Historical, Planning Board, etc.).
   5| 10. Establishes and manages effective preventive maintenance schedules, building control system optimization
   6| and analytics, and energy efficiency measures including warranty management of new and existing equipment
   7| and appliances.
   8| 11. Ensures that all town building systems are serviceable, including ventilation, air conditioning, heating,
   9| mechanical, electrical, refrigeration, monitoring and alarm systems.
  10| 12. Oversees the performance of various construction or repair work with contractors, vendors, suppliers and
  11| Town employees; evaluates construction documents and plans associated with the maintenance and repair of
  12| Town-owned buildings to ensure that projects are completed in accordance with specifications and within
  13| established budgets.
  14| 13. Ensures that the procurement of price quotations and supplies related to the Facilities and Grounds
  15| Department operations are in accordance with the State Procurement Law.
  16| 14. Responsible for coordinating with DPW director to ensure snow removal and winter safety procedures during
  17| and after storms provide timely access to town facilities.
  18| 15. Provides information and/or advice related to any building construction, repair, reuse, or disposal of town
  19| property.
  20| 16. Prepares and presents the operating budgets under this department; actively monitors utility and
  21| maintenance expenses to identify needed efficiencies and to advise the Town Manager of any changes in spending
  22| trends.
  23| 17. Engages in regular trainings and participates in trade shows and industry-events to remain current on
  24| technological advancements and best practices.
  25| The minimum qualifications that will be sought for this position include a Bachelor’s Degree, preferably in
  26| Construction Administration/Management, Business Administration, or Project Management. A qualified
  27| candidate will possess at least 5 years of supervisory experience and 10 years of combined industry experience
  28| related to construction, business management/energy management, and building system management. Essential
  29| skills include superior verbal and written communications, policy formulation, preparing and providing public
  30| presentations, and technological fluency in Microsoft Office and computerized maintenance management
  31| systems. Successful candidates will have a working knowledge of automated temperature control systems, HVAC,
  32| carpentry, electrical, plumbing and general building systems, and Energy Management Systems (EMS) and
  33| controls; or any equivalent combination of education, training and experience which provides the required
  34| knowledge, skills and abilities to perform the essential functions of the job. Must be knowledgeable about the
  35| International Building Code and OSHA requirements. Previous municipal employment is not required. The FY
  36| 2023 budgeted amount for this position is $85,000, plus benefits.

===PAGE 153=== (layout, 48 lines)
   1|                     5/7/22 ATM Article 22: Salary Administration Plan Bylaw Amendments
   2| § 70-1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE
   3| This by-law establishes a Salary Administration Plan, hereinafter called the “Plan”, which classifies several
   4| positions of town services into groups, establishes salary schedules, establishes so called                “fringe” benefits, or
   5| “indirect pay” provisions, such as holidays and vacations with pay, sick leave, and the like, and establishes a
   6| Personnel Committee to administer the Plan, as hereinafter provided, and to represent the interest of both
   7| taxpayers and town employees.
   8| § 70-2. PERSONNEL COMMITTEE
   9|       A. There is hereby established a Personnel Committee consisting of five members, each residing in
  10|           the Town of Lunenburg, to be appointed by the SelectmenSelect Board, and each member shall
  11|           serve for a term of three years; provided, however, that of the members originally appointed, one
  12|           shall serve a term of one year, two for a term of two years, and two for a term of three years, as
  13|           designated by the SelectmenSelect Board. However, nothing herein shall be deemed to prohibit
  14|           the chairman of the Personnel Committee from designating a member of said committee to serve
  15|           as a representative of said Committee on a study committee of the Town Meeting, nor shall such
  16|           service affect the      tenure of said member of the Personnel Committee. If any member of the
  17|           Personnel Committee shall resign or otherwise vacate the office before the expiration of their
  18|           term, the successor shall be appointed by the SelectmenSelect Board, at their discretion, to serve
  19|           for the balance of the unexpired term.
  20|       B. A Town employee may have a staff assignment to serve as a subject matter expert and non-voting
  21|           member of the Personnel Committee and may be called upon from time to time to provide
  22|           information requested by the Personnel Committee so that the Personnel Committee can make
  23|           well-informed decisions and proposals.
  24|       C. Said Committee shall elect its own chairman and vice-chair by September July 15th of each yearat
  25|           their first meeting after June 30th of each year; and in the absence or incapacity of the chairman,
  26|           the  vice-    chair  shall  act  as  chairman.      The  Committee  shall  keep  a  record  of  its  official
  27|           proceedings  and  actions,  may  establish  its  own  rule  of  procedure  and  may,  subject  to
  28|           appropriation  therefore,  retain  such  clerical  and  other  assistance,  and  make  such  other
  29|           expenditures as may be necessary to the performance of its duties. Three members shall constitute
  30|           a quorum for the transaction of the business of the committee, and the affirmative vote of three
  31|           members shall be necessary to any official act of the Committee.
  32|       D. The Personnel Committee is hereby authorized to administer the provisions of this by-law, except
  33|           for such duties as may be specifically assigned by statute , Town Charter or by-law to other Town
  34|           officers, boards or committees. Appeals for decisions of the Personnel Committee may be taken
  35|           to the Town at the next Annual or Special Town Meeting.
  36|       E. The Personnel Committee may from time to time issue, amend, or revoke Administrative Orders
  37|           for the purpose of giving effect to the provisions of this by-law, and for the purpose of giving
  38|           effect to the provisions of such other by-laws and votes of the Town, pertaining to personnel
  39|           administration, as said Committee may be responsible for administering and/or enforcing. Each
  40|           such order shall be numbered in sequence, and said Committee shall maintain in the Town Office
  41|           a file for all such orders issued, said file to be open to public inspection.
  42|       F. Except as otherwise provided by law, the Committee shall have access to all facts, figures, records
  43|           and other information relating to the personnel of Town departments, and the same shall be
  44|           furnished forthwith by any such department whenever so requested by the Personnel Committee,
  45|           in such a form  as said Committee may require.
  46|       G. In the event that the Personnel Committee does not have enough members to conduct business, the
  47|           Town Manager shall have the authority to administer the Salary Administration Plan.
  48|                                                                  153

===PAGE 154=== (layout, 39 lines)
   1| § 70-3. CLASSIFICATION AND SALARY SCHEDULE
   2| The positions of all employees in the service of the Town, and not otherwise covered by a collective
   3| bargaining agreement or employees of the school, are hereby classified by titles in the groups listed in
   4| Schedule A, entitled “Classification and Salary Schedule”, as set forth in this by-law.
   5| § 70-4. TITLE OF POSITIONS
   6| No person shall be appointed, employed or paid as an employee in any position subject to the provision of the
   7| Salary Administration Plan under any title other than those of the Classification and Salary Schedule, or under
   8| any title other than that of the job the duties of which are actually performed. The job title in the Classification
   9| and Salary Schedule of the Plan shall be the official title for all purposes having to do with the position and
  10| shall be used to designate the position in all payrolls, budget estimates and official reports, and in every other
  11| connection involving personnel and fiscal processes.
  12| § 70-5. NEW OR CHANGED POSITIONS
  13| The Personnel Committee tentatively may add new job title and descriptions to this Plan, establishing grades
  14| with minimum and maximum wage or salary brackets for the same, and reclassifying existing jobs, revising
  15| the description and the minimum and maximum wage and salary brackets therefore, and such addition or
  16| reclassification to be effective only for the period from the date of such action by the Personnel Committee to
  17| the date of any vote with respect thereto adopted at a subsequent Town Meeting but in no case later than the
  18| next Annual Town Meeting unless such later date as may be specified by such vote. Any increase in wages or
  19| salaries under this section shall be effective only if funds are available for the purpose, and approved by the
  20| Finance Committee and Board of SelectmenSelect Board.
  21| § 70-6. RECLASSIFICATION OF EMPLOYEES
  22| No employee shall be reclassified to a job in another group, either higher or lower, until the Personnel
  23| Committee shall have determined that such a reclassification will be consistent with the provisions of the
  24| Salary Administration Plan. Eligibility for a step increase of a reclassified employee will be granted one year
  25| from the date of reclassification.
  26| § 70-7. JOB DESCRIPTIONS AND INTERPRETATIONS
  27| A.  The Personnel Committee shall maintain written job descriptions of the jobs or positions in the Plan,
  28| describing the essential functions, requirements and duties. The descriptions shall not be interpreted as either
  29| complete  or  restrictive  and  employees  shall  continue  to  perform  any  duties  assigned  by  an  employee                      ’s
  30| supervisor.
  31| B. Every three (3) years, the Personnel committee shall review the wage and salary schedules of all positions
  32| in the Plan, and be in a position to recommend to the                 Board of SelectmenSelect Board            any action deemed
  33| desirable to maintain a fair and equitable pay level.
  34| § 70-8. SALARY SCHEDULE
  35| A. The Classification and Salary Schedule of the Salary Administration Plan, set forth in Schedule A of this by
  36| -law, shall consist of the maximum and minimum salaries and step                  -rate salaries for the groups and positions in
  37| the Classification and Salary Schedule. The salary range of a group shall be the salary range for all positions
  38| classified in such group.
  39|                                                                   154

===PAGE 155=== (layout, 40 lines)
   1|       B. Irrespective of the period of time for which pay rates may be shown in the Salary Schedule, all
   2|            employees shall continue to be paid on an hourly, weekly, monthly, or other basis, as at present,
   3|            unless  otherwise  authorized  by  the  Personnel  Committee  or  by  amendment  of  the  Salary
   4|            Administration Plan.
   5| §70-9. SALARY RATES ABOVE MAXIMUM
   6| Any salary rate whichrate that is above the maximum rate for a job, as established by Salary Administration
   7| Plan, shall be deemed to be a personal rate, and apply only to the incumbent. When such incumbent leaves the
   8| employ of the Town or is transferred to another job with a higher maximum rate than their personal rate or
   9| higher maximum rates are established, the personal rate shall disappear. No other  employee assigned to or
  10| hired for such job shall advance beyond the maximum of the job.
  11| §70-10. LAYOFF PROCEDURE; RECALL PROCEDURE
  12| A.  Layoff Procedure – If the Town decides to layoff employees, it shall determine from which classification
  13| (s)  or  job  function(s)  layoffs  shall  be  made.  Layoffs  shall  then be  made  based  on  seniority  within  the
  14| classification.
  15|      1) “Seniority” shall be the length of an employee’s continuous service measured from the employee                          ’s most
  16| recent appointment date; provided an employee             ’s seniority may be reduced  for periods of unpaid leave, unless
  17| otherwise prohibited by law.
  18|     2) If two or more employees in the same classification have the same seniority, the employee who has the
  19| shorter term of continuous employment with the Town shall be laid off. If there are two or more employees in
  20| the same classification with the same seniority and the same term of continuous employment with the Town, a
  21| part-time employee shall be laid off before a full          -time employee. If there are still two or more employees in the
  22| same classification, the Town  Manager shall determine which employee shall be laid                       off.
  23|    3)An employee that is scheduled to be laid off shall have the right to bump other employees in lower
  24| classifications with less seniority, provided that the Town Manager, in                    his/hertheir   sole discretion, but with
  25| consultation of the Department Head, shall make a determination that the employee retained is qualified and
  26| able to perform the duties of the lower classification, subject to disability discrimination                 laws.
  27|    4) Employees who have been laid off from employment are responsible for keeping the Town advised of
  28| their current address for the purpose of potential recall from            layoff.
  29| B. Recall Policy – If the town conducts a recall from layoff, recall shall be offered in reverse order of layoff by
  30| classification provided the laid off employee is qualified and able to perform the duties of the classification to
  31| which the employee is being offered recall, subject to disability discrimination laws. Employees shall only be
  32| eligible for recall from layoff under the terms of this policy for one (1) year from the date of the employee                          ’s
  33| layoff.
  34| 1)  Any employee who is recalled from layoff under the terms of this policy shall retain                       his/hertheir seniority,
  35| less the period of layoff.
  36| 2) The Town will notify an employee of a recall opportunity by certified letter (or other verifiable method of
  37| delivery) to the employee’s last known address. An employee who has been offered an opportunity to be
  38| recalled from layoff must advise the Town of               his/hertheir intention to accept  recall within five (5) working
  39| days.
  40|                                                                   155

===PAGE 156=== (layout, 40 lines)
   1| § 70-11. TRANSFERS AND PROMOTIONS
   2| A.  When an employee is promoted to a job with a higher rate range, or rate of pay, he/she shall enter it at the
   3| minimum of the job rate range or at their own rate, whichever is the higher, but in no event at a rate in excess
   4| of the maximum for such job.Upon promotion, the employee will be placed on the pay grade for the new
   5| position at the lowest step at which the employee would receive a pay increase.                     They may also receive at the
   6| time one or more step rate increases, provided the maximum for the job is not exceeded, if the Department
   7| Head recommends that qualification and performance warrant it, and the Personnel Committee approves. If the
   8| Department Head should feel that there should be a trial period before recommending the promotional step rate
   9| increase or increases, on recommendation of the Department Head, the Personnel Committee may approve any
  10| such deferred promotional increase at the conclusion of the trial             period.
  11| B.  f an employee is transferred to a job with a lower rate range, or rate of pay, they shall enter it at                     the pay
  12| rate closest to their current rate   their own rate or at the maximum rate for the job, whichever is the  lower.
  13| § 70-12. NEW PERSONNEL
  14| A.  The hiring rate shall be the minimum of the rate range of the job for which the new employee                           if is hired,
  15| unless   otherwise  authorized  by  the  Personnel  Committee.               the  Town  Manager  recommends  paying  the
  16| employee at a higher step due to the employee’s qualifications and experience or marketing conditions. The
  17| Personnel Committee must vote to approve any recommendation above step 1. Department Heads shall notify
  18| the Town Manager of the hiring of all new personnel. This notification shall be done in writing via the New
  19| Hire Personnel Form.
  20| B.  Effective July 1, 2015, for new regular full          -time and new regular part        -time personnel for whose position
  21| there is a rate rangest , eligibility for a step increase will depend on the employeeth          ’s date of hire. If hired between st
  22| January 1     and June 30     , the employee is not eligible for a step increase until July 1            of the following calendar
  23| year. If hired between July 1st and December 31st, the employee is eligible for their first step increase on the
  24| upcoming July 1st.
  25| C. Effective July 1, 2022, for new regular full          -time and new regular part-time personnel, eligibility for a step
  26| increase will be on the anniversary date of employment.
  27| § 70-13. GRIEVANCE PROCEDURE
  28| A.  Any employee who believes that they have been received inequitable treatmentsubject to a violation of the
  29| Salary Administration Plan         is encouraged to discuss the         grievance initially with their supervisor within 10
  30| working days of the occurrence or knowledge of the event. If the employee is  not  comfortable discussing the
  31| matter with their direct supervisor, or if the matter is not settled, the employee should submit the said
  32| grievance to their Department Head. The Department Head shall meet with the employee and will respond in
  33| writing to the employee within 14 days of the meeting.
  34| B.  If the grievance is not resolved by the decision of the Department Head, the employee may                            escalate the
  35| grievance to the Assistant Town Manager/HR Director. If unsatisfied, the employee may                             file an appeal in
  36| writing with the Town Manager within            10 days of receipt of     the most recent decision. The Town Manager will
  37| meet with the employee within a reasonable time after receipt of the appeal and will respond in writing to the
  38| employee’s grievance within 30 days after meeting with the employee.                    The Town Manager’s decision shall be
  39| final and binding.

   6| In the event any provision of this by-law or application thereof,                  shall be    held to be invalid by the proper

  10| Nothing in this by-law of Salary Administration Plan shall be construed to conflict with Chapter 31 (Civil
  11| Service) of the General Laws.
  12| § 70-17. HOURS OF WORK; OVERTIME; COMPENSATORY TIME
  13| A. Non-Exempt Employees:
  14| The schedule of hours to be worked shall be determined by the appropriate Department Head.
  15|                1) For   non-exempt      positions entitled to     overtime pay at one and one-half (1 1/2) times the
  16|                     regular rate for     work actually performed          in excess of forty (40) hours within a             work
  17|                     weekworkweek, . the The words “work actually performed” shall be construed to include
  18|                     all duly paid time off, including but not limited to holidays, sick leave, vacation, personal
  19|                     days and bereavement  leave, and such leave shall be included in the computation of forty
  20|                     (40) hours.
  21|                2) Unauthorized leave or leave for which the employee is not entitled to compensation shall
  22|                     not be included in such computation, unless otherwise required by law.
  23|  B. Hours by occupation.
  24|  1. Industrial Trades and Service        Occupations
  25|            a..Work Week 40 hours in 5         days.
  26|            b. One and one-half (1 1/2) times the regular rate will be paid for work actually performed                    in
  27|                  excess of forty (40) hours within a work week.
  28|  2. Office Occupations
  29|            a. Work Week - Forty (40) hours in five days and may be reduced administratively to 36 hours in
  30|                   four or five days.
  31|            b. One and one-half (1 1/2) times the regular rate will be paid for work actually performed in
  32|                  excess of forty (40) hours within a work week.
  33|  3. Public Safety Occupations
  34|            Fire Department - The hours of the Fire Department shall be determined by the Chief with the
  35|   approval of the Personnel Committee provided, however, that all hours worked by any member
  36|   of the Fire Department will be paid for at the regular rate then in force.
  37| B. Exempt Employees:
  38|       Exempt Employees, Administrative and Professional Occupations, are not governed by hours of the
  39|     positions under their supervision or control. Generally, such positions shall be for at least forty (40)
  40|     hours per week when full-time and not entitled to overtime.

   3| Persons employed in these positions are not governed by hours of the positions under their

   6| C. Notwithstanding anything in the foregoing paragraphs, the appropriate department head  or his/
   7| her designee  may, instead of  paying the overtime rate, provide that the employee take
   8| compensatory time off at a rate of 1 1/2 times the hours worked that qualify for overtime pay.

  10| § 70-18. PAID VACATIONS
  11| A. All persons employed regularly by the Town full -time or part-time for a minimum of twenty
  12| (20) hours per week shall receive paid vacations according to the following schedule. Any
  13| persons working between 20 and 40 hours per week shall receive paid vacations, but it will be
  14| prorated accordingly. The  part-time compensation vacation and sick leave  formula shall be
  15| used to determine the prorated  part- time employee ’s vacation day (See  SECTION 28.
  16| DEFINITIONS Section 28).
  17| B. Earned vacation time up to and
  18| including 80 hours must be used in the year granted. No unused portion of this allowance shall be
  19| carried forward into a subsequent vacation year, nor shall any monetary compensation be given
  20| for such unused portion. Earned vacation time in excess of 80 hours should be used in the year
  21| granted, and no portion of such excess shall be carried forward into a subsequent year. If
  22| departmental scheduling or other serious extenuating circumstances preclude the  use of all such
  23| excess in the year granted, the employee may request to receive compensation equivalent to such
  24| unused portion in a lump sum payable on the first payroll warrant in July, in addition to their
  25| regular salary, or may be allowed to carry over such excess as the Town may elect in its sole
  26| discretion. Any employee asserting a serious extenuating circumstance must submit a written
  27| request to  his/hertheir direct supervisor and such request must be approved by the direct
  28| supervisor and the Town Manager before the employee shall be compensated for, or allowed to
  29| carry over, such excess.
  30| C. Employment Termination. An employee whose employment ends , whether voluntary
  31| resignation, retirement or involuntary termination,  shall receive be paid for all accrued, but
  32| unused vacation

  23| § 70-19. PAID HOLIDAYS
  24| A. The Town of Lunenburg shall observe the following holidays:
  25|         New Year’s Day                                                     Labor Day
  26|       Martin Luther King Day                                     Columbus Day
  27|       Presidents Day                                             Veterans’ Day
  28|       Patriots Day                                               Thanksgiving Day
  29|       Memorial Day                                               One Floating Holiday*
  30|       Juneteenth                                                 Day Before Christmas
  31|       Independence Day                                           Christmas Day
  32|  B. Employees who regularly work forty (40) hours per week, and/or five (5) days per week shall receive
  33|  paid holiday leave on the day on the Town observes the holiday based on the base hourly rate of the
  34|  employee for  the number of hours the employee is regularly scheduled to work on the day of the week
  35|  on which the holiday is observed.
  36| B. C. Employees who regularly work less than forty (40) hours and/or five (5) days per week shall receive paid
  37| holiday leave on the day the Town observes the holiday provided the employee is regularly scheduled to work
  38| on the day the Town observes the holiday, based on the hourly rate of the employee for the number of hours
  39| the employee is regularly scheduled to work on the day of the week on which the holiday is observed.
  40| C.  D. Holiday leave will be granted on the day the Town observes the holiday, when possible. If the holiday
  41| falls on a Saturday, it will be observed on the preceding Friday. If the holiday falls on a Sunday, it will be
  42| observed on the following Monday. If an eligible employee is required to work on the day the Town observes
  43| the holiday, they will be entitled to a Floating Day, which has to be used within 30 days, by agreement of the
  44| supervisor and employee. Employees shall receive a floating holiday* for any holiday that falls on a day that
  45| they  are   not  regularly    scheduled    to  work.    If the   department     head   determines     that  holiday    leave   cannot
  46| be
  47|                                                                  159

===PAGE 160=== (plain, 44 lines)
   1| 160
   2| granted, the employee shall receive payment at one and one-half times the base hourly rate for all hours worked
   3| on the holiday, in addition to his or her holiday pay. Final determination is to be made by the Town Manager.
   4| B. E. To be eligible for paid holiday leave, an employee must work his or her full assigned shift on his or her
   5| regularly scheduled workday before a holiday, and the next regularly scheduled working day follow ing the
   6| holiday, unless the employee has been excused for a legitimate reason by the direct supervisor.
   7| * Floating Holiday time for employees who work less than 40 hours per week will be calculated in hours and
   8| determined by dividing the number of hours regularly worked per week, by the number of days they work in  one
   9| week.
  10| § 70-20. SICK LEAVE
  11| A. All persons employed, full -time or part -time who have completed the six (6) months  probationary
  12| waiting period by July 1 of any year, shall be entitled to sick leave of ninety-six (96) hours, to be credited
  13| on July 1. The  part-time compensationVacation and Sick Leave formula shall be used to determine the
  14| employee’s prorated sick pay (See SECTION 28. DEFINITIONSSection 28).
  15| B. New employees having completed the six (6) months probationarywaiting period shall be credited as
  16| of the end of said six (6) months, with sick leave in the amount of eight (8) hours for each full month
  17| intervening between the six months anniversary date and the next July 1, up to the maximum o f ninety-
  18| six (96) hours. Use of such leave must be occasioned by sickness or injury of the employee or their
  19| immediate family member.
  20| C. Unused sick leave shall be cumulative up to a maximum of 1,200 hours, and shall be carried forward
  21| on July 1 of each yea r, to be added to the sick leave credited as of July 1.  The vacation and sick leave
  22| formula shall be used to determine the employee’s prorated maximum sick hours (See SECTION 28.
  23| DEFINITIONS). Sick leave may be donated to another employee who has exhausted  their accumulated
  24| sick leave at the discretion of the Town Manager.
  25| D. When an employee finds it necessary to be absent because of accident or illness, they shall report the
  26| fact to their immediate supervisor as soon as possible either in person or by agent. Sick leave will not  be
  27| granted unless such report is made. For the protection of the Town, the Department Head or supervisor
  28| may require the presentation of a doctor’s certificate in connection with a claim for sick  leave that
  29| exceeds, or is equal to 24 hours (or three missed days of work)., and may, if it is deemed advisable, send
  30| a doctor or nurse to investigate any absence alleged to be caused by illness . Any employee who refuses
  31| to submit to a doctor’s examination note shall not be entitled to sick leave.
  32| E. An employee who retires in good standing shall receive compensation based on a percentage of
  33| his/hertheir accumulated unused sick leave, up to a maximum of 480 hours, as follows: 5 years continuous
  34| service 10%; 15 years continuous service 50%; 25  years continuous service 75%. In the event of an
  35| employee’s death, the above benefit will be paid to  his/hertheir beneficiary. This retirement benefit will
  36| not be available to any employee hired after June 30, 2010.
  37| F. Sick Time can be used for any of the following reasons:
  38| 1) Care for the employee’s own physical or mental illness, injury or medical condition that requires
  39| home care or rest, professional medical diagnosis or care, or preventative medical care;
  40| 2) Care for the employee’s child, spouse, parent or parent of a spouse, who is suffering from a
  41| physical or mental illness, injury, or medical condition that requires home care, professional
  42| medical diagnosis or care, or preventive medical care;
  43| 3) Attending the employee’s routine medical appointment or a routine medical appointment for  the
  44| employee’s child, spouse, parent, or parent of spouse.

===PAGE 161=== (plain, 39 lines)
   1| 161
   2| § 70-21. PERSONAL LEAVE
   3| A. All persons employed, full time or part time who have completed six (6) months probationary waiting
   4| period by July 1 of any year, shall be entitled to personal leave of three (3) days twenty-four (24) hours,
   5| to become available on July 1 for use during the ensuing fiscal year. The part-time compensation formula
   6| shall be used to determine the employee’s prorated personal hours (See Section 26).
   7| B. New employees having completed the six (6) months probationarywaiting period shall be credited as
   8| of the end of said six (6) months, with eight one (81) personal hours day for use during the period between
   9| said six (6) months anniversary and the end of the then current fiscal year; provided, however, that the
  10| said six (6) months probationarywaiting period must have been completed by May 1 of that year.
  11| C. Employees who do not use any sick leave within a three month ( 3) month period (calculated
  12| quarterly), will be entitled to  eight one (81) additional personal  hours day for each such quarter; said
  13| personal hours days are to be used in the same fiscal year in which they are earned.  Personal leave  shall
  14| be scheduled with the approval of the Department Head and/or his/hertheir designee. Personal leave shall
  15| not be cumulative.
  16| § 70-22. FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE
  17| A. The Town agrees to abide by the provisions of the 1993 Family and Medical Leave Act (FMLA). All eligible
  18| employees are entitled to up to twelve (12) weeks of unpaid leave for certain family and medical reasons in a one
  19| (1) year  period.  Employees are eligible for FMLA if they have worked for the Town for at least one (1) year and
  20| at least for one thousand two hundred and fifty (1,250) hours over the previous twelve (12) months.
  21| B. Unpaid FMLA leave must be granted for any of the following reasons:
  22| 1. To care for the employee’s child after birth, or placement for adoption or foster care
  23| 2. To care for the employee’s spouse, son, daughter, or parent, who has a serious health condition
  24| 3. For a serious health condition that makes the employee unable to perform the employee’s job
  25| C. In accordance with the Town’s policy, the Town requires the use of paid leave time during the FMLA absence
  26| including accumulated Sick Leave, Vacation Leave, and Personal Leave. The Town requires all employees
  27| applying for an FMLA leave to notify their Department Head, in writing, thirty (30) days prior to the  leave, if
  28| possible.provides leave in accordance with the Family and Medical Leave  Act  (FMLA)  and  its FMLA Policy,
  29| as adopted by the Select Board and as it may be amended from time-to-time. A copy of the Town’s FMLA Policy
  30| is available on the Town’s website.
  31| § 70-23. BEREAVEMENT LEAVE
  32| In the event of death in an employee’s family (wife, husband, mother (in-law), father (in-law), child, grandparents,
  33| brother, or sister) up to and including three days off duty may be granted, with pay, not to be charged against sick
  34| leave. (spouse, children, parents, in-laws, step-children, step-parents, significant other or someone who is under
  35| your direct care and living in your home, up to and including five (5) days off duty shall be granted, with pay. In
  36| the event of death of a grandparent, grandchild, sibling, sibling-in law, step-sibling, uncle, aunt, nephew, niece,
  37| three (3) days off duty shall be granted, with pay. Two (2) additional days off, with pay, may be granted at the
  38| discretion of the Town Manager not to be charged against sick leave. Any time  above these days is to be charged
  39| against sick leave.

===PAGE 162=== (plain, 39 lines)
   1| 162
   2| § 70-24. MILITARY LEAVE
   3| The Town provides military leave in accordance with state and federal law.
   4| A. Town employees with a minimum of six consecutive month’s employment with the Town who serve in the State
   5| or Federal military training forces under orders shall be allowed full pay for not more than one normal working week
   6| and shall be granted a military leave of absence for the period of their required service with said forces.
   7| B. Military leave of absence with pay shall be granted to employees of the Town on occasion of their  appearance
   8| before local draft boards or draft appeal boards, or for physical examinations ordered by said  boards; but no such leave
   9| shall be granted for a period of more than one day without the approval of the Personnel Committee. Such leave shall
  10| be deemed to be a military leave of absence.
  11| C. A military leave of absence without pay shall be granted to any Town Officer or employee called to active  duty
  12| with the State or Federal Armed Forces for purposes other than the routine annual tour of duty for training purposes.
  13| D. As used herein, the phrases “military training forces” or “armed forces” shall mean the United States Air Force, the
  14| United States Army, the United States Navy, the Unites States Marine Corps, the United States Coast Guard, the
  15| Massachusetts National Guard, the Massachusetts Air National Guard, the Massachusetts State Guard, and any and all
  16| components thereof.
  17| § 70-25. JURY DUTY
  18| All persons permanently employed, whether full -time or part -time, for a minimum of twenty (20) hours per  week,
  19| called for jury duty shall be paid by the Town, an amount equal to the difference between the compensation paid for a
  20| normal working period and the amount paid by the court, excluding allowance for travel, and this will be certified by
  21| the Town Clerk or Treasurer upon presentation of the check for monies received for Jury Duty. Jury duty pay is for up
  22| to three days of pay.
  23| § 70-26. LONGEVITY PAY
  24| After reaching ten (10) years of employment, and thereafter, each employee permanently employed, whether  full or
  25| part time, shall receive an annual bonus equal to 2% of their annual salary, and is payable in the first pay period after
  26| the employee’s anniversary date, at 15 years of continuous service – 2.5% of their annual salary,  and at 20 years of
  27| continuous service – 3% of their annual salary.
  28| § 70-27. TRAVEL REIMBURSEMENT
  29| Travel reimbursement shall be by signed voucher at a rate established by the IRS when using the employee’s own car,
  30| plus tolls and parking fees.
  31| § 70-28. DISCIPLINE POLICY
  32| A. Items which constitute grounds for disciplinary action against an employee include: chronic absenteeism or
  33| tardiness, insubordination or refusal to work, infr action of work and safety rules, threat of physical violence against
  34| fellow employees, use of profane language to a supervisor or fellow employee, or offenses of comparable gravity or
  35| severity.
  36| B. Process.
  37| 1) Upon determination by the Department Head that disci plinary action is warranted, the following guidelines
  38| shall be implemented for all employees that have successfully completed a six (6) month probationary waiting
  39| period:

===PAGE 163=== (plain, 44 lines)
   1| 163
   2| STEP 1. 1st Offense - Written warning.
   3| STEP 2. 2nd Offense - A formal written reprimand notifying the employee of a one -day suspension and
   4| that one further offense shall result in a three -day suspension without pay.
   5| STEP 3. 3rd Offense - Employee will receive an immediate three -day suspension without pay and a
   6| written notification that any further offense shall be grounds for dismissal.
   7| STEP 4. 4th Offense - Immediate dismissal of the employee.
   8| 1) Depending on the particular circumstances, one or more of the above steps may be skipped. The offense
   9| for which disciplinary action is taken may be any combination of the items described above. For example,
  10| offense number one could be tardiness, offense number two could be absenteeism,  and offense number
  11| three could be refusal to work or insubordination. If an employee goes twenty-four
  12| (24) months without a further offense, the first offense beyond twenty -four (24) months shall be
  13| disciplined at the level of step one.
  14| 2) The following infractions are grounds for the immediate dismissal of an employee for just cause by the
  15| Department Head. Just cause may include, but shall not be limited to, the following:
  16| 1) Willful neglect or non-performance of one or more assigned duties;
  17| 2) Demonstrated incompetence in the performance of one or more assigned duties;
  18| 3) Behavior that seriously interferes with the normal operation of the municipality, the department, or any
  19| member of the workforce:
  20| 4) Insubordination, which shall mean a refusal to carry out a direct order;
  21| 5) Dishonesty in the performance of assigned duties:
  22| 6) Chronic absenteeism or tardiness without reasonable excuse;
  23| 7) Unauthorized possession or use of alcohol or an unprescribed controlled substance during any period of
  24| assigned work;
  25| 8) Municipal theft.
  26| SECTION 28. DEFINITIONS
  27| The following terms used in the Salary Administration Plan shall be defined as set forth herein:
  28| Full-Time Employee shall mean any person employee who spends an amount of time between is regularly
  29| scheduled to work thirty-six (36) and forty (40) hours of permanently scheduled work per week in the service of
  30| the Townhours or more per week.
  31| Hourly Employee shall mean any employee of the Town who by virtue of their classified position is compensated
  32| at an hourly rate for services rendered.
  33| Intermittent Employee shall mean any person employee who works less than tweis regularly scheduled to work
  34| fewer than twenty (20) hours per week on a regular basis. Intermittent employees are not eligible for benefits (i.e.
  35| vacation, sick-time, holiday, longevity, insurance, unless required by law etc.) and work less than one thousand
  36| forty (1,040) hours per year in the service of the Town.
  37| Part Time Compensation Vacation and Sick Leave Formula shall be determined by dividing the compensation or
  38| time off by 40 and then multiplying by the number of hours the employee works per week.  For example, a  40
  39| hour per week employee is eligible for 120 hours of vacation. If another employee with the same tenure works 36
  40| hours, the formula is as follows: 120 / 40 = 3. Then multiply 3 x 36 hours = 108 hours.
  41| Part-Time Employee shall mean any person employee who spends is regularly scheduled to work at least twenty
  42| (20) hours and fewerless than thirty-six (36) hours but twenty (20) hours or more of permanently scheduled work
  43| per week in the service of the Town.
  44| ProbationaryWaiting Period shall mean the initial six (6) month period of employment with the Town.

===PAGE 164=== (layout, 43 lines)
   1|  Seasonal Employee shall mean any employee, part or full-time who spends works no more than six (6)
   2|  months180 days or one hundred eighty (180) days whichever is less per year., in the service of the Town.
   3|  Seasonal employees are not eligible for benefits (i.e. vacation, sick-time, holiday, longevity, insurance,
   4|  etc.unless required by law).

   6| ARTICLE 30.  To see if the Town will vote to adopt the following “Resolution in Support of Changing the
   7| State Flag & Seal of Massachusetts”:
   8| Whereas the history of Massachusetts is replete with instances of conflict between the European Colonists and
   9| the Native Nations of the region, who first extended the hand of friendship to the Colonists on their shores in
  10| 1620, and helped them to survive starvation during the settlers first winters on their land;
  11| Whereas members of the Native Nation for who the State of Massachusetts is named were ambushed and
  12| killed by Myles Standish, first commander of the Plymouth Colony, in April of 1623, barely two years after
  13| the Pilgrims arrived on their shores;
  14| Whereas the Colonial broadsword brandished above the head of the Native man on the Massachusetts State
  15| Flag and Seal is modeled after Myles Standish        ’s own broadsword, borrowed for that purpose from the Pilgrim
  16| Hall in Plymouth by the illustrator Edmund Garrett in 1884;
  17| Whereas the belt binding the Native      ’s cloak on the Flag and Seal is modeled after a belt worn by Metacomet,
  18| known to the English as King Philip, who was among the Wampanoag leaders who resorted to a mutually
  19| destructive war in 1675-76 in defense of Native lands against Colonial encroachment;
  20| Whereas the proportions of the body of the Native man in the Flag and Seal were modeled from a Native
  21| skeleton kept in Winthrop, the bow modeled after a bow taken from a Native man shot and killed by a colonist
  22| in Sudbury in 1665, and his facial features taken from a photograph of an Ojibwe chief from Great Falls,
  23| Montana, considered by the illustrator to be a      “fine specimen of an Indian,    ” though not from Massachusetts;
  24| Whereas the history of relations between Massachusetts since Colonial times and the Native Nations who
  25| continue to live within its borders includes the forced internment of thousands of so           -called “praying Indians”
  26| on Deer Island, in Boston Harbor, where they died by the hundreds of exposure in 1675, their subsequent
  27| enslavement in Boston, Bermuda, and the Caribbean Islands, the offering of 40 pounds sterling as bounty for
  28| the scalps of Native men, women and children in Massachusetts beginning in 1686, increased to 100 pounds
  29| sterling for the scalps of Native adult males by 1722, half that amount for Native women and children;
  30| Whereas Native Americans were legally prohibited from even stepping foot into Boston from 1675 until 2004,
  31| when that law was finally repealed;
  32|                    th
  33| Whereas the 400      anniversary of the landing of the colonists at Plymouth Plantation in the year 2020, afforded
  34| every citizen of the Commonwealth a chance to reflect upon this history and come to an awareness of the
  35| possibility of a better relationship between the descendants of the Colonial immigrants and the Native Nations
  36| of the Commonwealth;
  37| Whereas Native Americans have long suffered the many abuses of racism, the appropriation of their symbols
  38| for public schools and sports teams, the loss of their ancestral lands and the encroachment of their cultural
  39| lifeways;
  40| Whereas the area now known as the Town of Lunenburg shares a rich Native history with modern day tribal
  41| Nations, who inhabited this area long before the first colonial settlers arrived circa 1718, such as the Abenaki
  42| and  the  Nipmuc;    Now,  therefore,  BE  IT  RESOLVED  that  the  Town  of  Lunenburg  hereby  adopts  this
  43| resolution  in  support  of  the  work  of  the  Special  Commission  Relative  to  the  Seal  and  Motto  of  the
  44| Commonwealth, established by a Resolve of the General Court in 2020 and appointed by the Governor to
  45|                                                              168

===PAGE 169=== (layout, 49 lines)
   1| recommend changes to the current flag and seal of Massachusetts; and in support of a new seal and motto for
   2| the Commonwealth that may better reflect our aspirations for harmonious and respectful relations between all
   3| people who now call Massachusetts home. The town clerk shall forward a copy of this resolution to Sen. Marc
   4| Pacheco and Rep. Antonio Cabral, co-chairs of the Joint Committee on State Administration, who serve as
   5| members of the Special Commission Relative to the Seal and Motto of the Commonwealth, and to Sen. John
   6| Cronin, Rep. Michael Kushmerek and Rep. Danillo Sena, with the request they support the work of the
   7| aforementioned   Special   Commission.   (Submitted   by   Citizen   Petition)   FINANCE   COMMITTEE
   8| RECOMMENDATION: No Direct Financial Impact, SELECT BOARD recommend Approval. VOTED (Yes-
   9| 48, No-20) to adopt “Resolution in Support of Changing the State Flag & Seal of Massachusetts”.

  47|                         design submittal and review.

===PAGE 172=== (layout, 52 lines)
   1|                   C.          Upon receipt of the Application the APDC shall review and hold a
   2|                             published hearing on the application. Said hearing to be noticed by an ad
   3|                             published in a newspaper with local circulation 14 days or more before the
   4|                             hearing. Within 45 days of the application submittal, a copy of which shall
   5|                             be filed with the Town Clerk, the APDC shall hold a public hearing on the
   6|                             application, said hearing to be noticed by an ad published at least once in a
   7|                             newspaper with local circulation 14 days or more before the hearing.
   8|                             Following the hearing, the APDC shall determine whether or not the
   9|                             proposed alteration is consistent with the standards set forth in § 113-9 and
  10|                             the associated design guidelines.         If the APDC determines by majority vote
  11|                             that the alteration adheres to the standards and the design guidelines, it
  12|                             shall issue a certificate to alter, which may be subject to reasonable
  13|                             conditions that the APDC deems necessary and appropriate. If the APDC
  14|                             does not achieve a majority vote that the alteration is compatible with the
  15|                             standards and the design guidelines, it shall make a negative
  16|                             determination. The APDC’s written determination shall be submitted to
  17|                             the property owner after within 20 days of the close of the public hearing,
  18|                             and no later than 75 90 days from after the receipt submittal of an
  19|                             application, unless granted a time extension by the owner. In When
  20|                             making its determination, the APDC shall provide a rationale for its action,
  21|                             including    a description    of how the alteration does or does not meet the
  22|                             standards set forth in § 113      -9 and the design guidelines. If the APDC
  23|                             denies a certificate to alter, it may include nonbinding, advisory only
  24|                             recommendations on how the proposed alterations may be modified to
  25|                             satisfy the APDC. Once the applicant            modifies   the alterations in a manner
  26|                             that the APDC finds acceptable, the APDC shall issue a certificate to alter.
  27|                             The determinations of the APDC relative to alterations described in this §
  28|                             113-8, whether positive or negative, shall be binding on the applicant.
  29|       and

  36|                          written copy of its actions to         the property owner and         shall file other copies
  37|                          with the office of the Town Clerk and the Building Commissioner. The date
  38|                          of issuance of a certificate or negative determination shall be the date of its

  47|                          of the property owner.

===PAGE 184=== (plain, 33 lines)
   1| 184
   2| The 1st Middlesex Representative race, which included Precincts A, C, D and sub precinct B1 had Mr.
   3| Shepherd ahead in Lunenburg but the district tally had Ms.Scarsdale ahead by 17 votes – this triggered a
   4| recount.  On December 10, 2022 Lunenburg conducted the recount in the Passios Building Cafeteria.
   5| Townsend Town Clerk, Kathleen Spofford and Pepperell Town Clerk, Brynn Durno, who had already
   6| completed their own recount for this election showed up and said we’re helping! They came in and took over
   7| the preparation of the precinct blocks. Their expertise and that of Town Counsel, Adam Costa made the entire
   8| process much less stressful for all.  The Registrars and I would like to thank all those who volunteered their
   9| time on a Saturday to get this job done. The total difference between the two candidates ended up being seven
  10| votes district wide.
